# SSOT-11: SECTION TYPE DEFINITIONS

## PURPOSE
Defines each section type (A1-A7, B1-B8) within the curriculum structure, specifying content requirements, image needs, and learning objectives.

## CORE PRINCIPLE: Geometric Element Theory
Every section connects to the theory: `TIMELESS → CULTURAL EXPRESSION → TECHNOLOGICAL INNOVATION → MODERN APPLICATION`.
- **Day A (Metaphor Focus):** Element as Sacred/Cultural Meaning.
- **Day B (Function Focus):** Element as Mathematical/Scientific Operation.

## DAY A: METAPHOR & CULTURE

### A1: MYTH HOOK
**Duration:** 5-7 mins
**Purpose:** Embed geometric element in sacred narrative.
**Content:** Narrative retelling, deity intro, element reveal.
**Image Needs:**
- **STORYBOARD:** 3-5 scenes (Opening, Conflict, Resolution).
- **MUSEUM_SEARCH:** 1-2 authentic deity depictions (e.g., Cylinder Seal).

### A2: SEL CONNECTION
**Duration:** 3-5 mins
**Purpose:** Internalize metaphor through emotion.
**Content:** Metaphor statement, discussion prompt, SEL skill.
**Image Needs:**
- **MUSEUM_SEARCH:** Artifact showing element in emotional context.
- **DIAGRAM (Optional):** Metaphor-feeling connection icon.

### A3: SYMBOLIC CHAIN
**Duration:** 5-7 mins
**Purpose:** Trace element from form to societal impact.
**Chain:** `Element → Property → Metaphor → Deity → Domain`.
**Image Needs:**
- **DIAGRAM:** Visual flow of the chain.
- **MUSEUM_SEARCH:** Element in domain context (e.g., legal stele).

### A4: DEITY ICONOGRAPHY
**Duration:** 5-7 mins
**Purpose:** Visual literacy - recognizing deities.
**Content:** Identifying features, semiotic meaning, element location.
**Image Needs:**
- **MUSEUM_SEARCH:** 3-5 distinct artifacts (Seals, Reliefs).
- **OVERLAY:** Annotated image highlighting features.

### A5: MATERIAL CULTURE
**Duration:** 7-10 mins
**Purpose:** Survey element across 6 artifact categories:
1. Monumental (Temples)
2. Administrative (Seals)
3. Devotional (Statues)
4. Funerary (Tombs)
5. Personal (Jewelry)
6. Astronomical (Calendars)
**Image Needs:**
- **MUSEUM_SEARCH:** 1-2 examples per category.

### A6: SEMIOTICS
**Duration:** 5-7 mins
**Purpose:** Decode visual meaning (Sign/Signifier/Signified).
**Image Needs:**
- **OVERLAY:** 2-3 annotated analyses.
- **MUSEUM_SEARCH:** Comparative symbol usage.

### A7: RITUAL PRACTICES
**Duration:** 5-7 mins
**Purpose:** Element in sacred and daily life.
**Image Needs:**
- **MUSEUM_SEARCH:** Ritual scenes (Ceremonial & Daily).
- **STORYBOARD (Optional):** Reconstructed scene if artifacts missing.

---

## DAY B: FUNCTION & INNOVATION

### B1: REVIEW & TRANSITION
**Duration:** 5 mins
**Purpose:** Bridge Metaphor -> Function.
**Image Needs:**
- **MUSEUM_SEARCH:** Callback artifact from Day A.

### B2: MATHEMATICAL PROPERTIES
**Duration:** 10-12 mins
**Purpose:** Define element mathematically (Parts, Formulas).
**Image Needs:**
- **DIAGRAM:** 3-5 specs (Labeled parts, Proofs, Formulas).
- **MUSEUM_SEARCH:** Historical math tablet/text.

### B3: SCIENCE/ENGINEERING
**Duration:** 10-12 mins
**Purpose:** Element enabling technology.
**Image Needs:**
- **MUSEUM_SEARCH:** Engineering artifacts (Models, Tools).
- **DIAGRAM:** Physics/Mechanics explanation (Forces, Flow).

### B4: TRANSFORMATIONS
**Duration:** 8-10 mins
**Purpose:** Geometric operations (Rotation, Reflection, etc.).
**Image Needs:**
- **DIAGRAM:** 4-6 Sequence/Step-by-step visualizations.
- **MUSEUM_SEARCH:** Pattern/Tessellation artifacts.

### B5: INVENTIONS
**Duration:** 8-10 mins
**Purpose:** Causal link: Element -> Invention.
**Image Needs:**
- **MUSEUM_SEARCH:** Ancient invention examples.
- **DIAGRAM:** Invention decomposition/Exploded view.

### B6: HISTORICAL CAUSATION
**Duration:** 5-7 mins
**Purpose:** Big picture impact & modern legacy.
**Image Needs:**
- **DIAGRAM:** Causal chain/Timeline.
- **STOCK/PHOTO:** Modern parallel technology.

### B7: DESIGN CHALLENGE
**Duration:** 15-20 mins
**Purpose:** Application of learning.
**Image Needs:**
- **PROCESS:** Step-by-step activity photos.

### B8: REVELATION
**Duration:** 5-10 mins
**Purpose:** Compare student work to historical solution.
**Image Needs:**
- **MUSEUM_SEARCH:** The "Answer" artifact.

---

# SSOT-12: DEITY DESIGN & SPECIFICATIONS

## OVERVIEW
Definitions for the visual representation of Mesopotamian deities across all media (Artifacts, Storyboards, Diagrams).
**Critical Rule:** All depictions must adhere to iconographic consistency.

## DEITY REGISTRY

### 1. SHAMASH (Sun God)
*Domain: Justice, Truth, Sun* | *Element: Circle*
- **Primary Symbol:** Sun Disk (Circle with 4/8 rays/wavy lines).
- **Iconography:** Saw-toothed knife, Rod & Ring, Horned crown, Tiered robe.
- **Colors:** Gold (#FFD700), Warm Yellow, Deep Blue.
- **Key Poses:** Rising between mountains (Dawn), Enthroned (Justice).
- **Availability Verdict:** ✅ **HIGH.** Excellent museum artifacts exist (e.g., Tablet of Shamash).

### 2. ISHTAR (Venus/War/Love)
*Domain: War, Love, Venus* | *Element: Triangle (8-pointed star)*
- **Primary Symbol:** 8-Pointed Star.
- **Iconography:** Lions (flanking/standing on), Weapons (mace/bow), Wings (optional), Horned crown.
- **Colors:** Lapis Blue (#1E4D8C), Red (#B22222), Gold.
- **Key Poses:** "Queen of Heaven" (Frontal, winged), Warrior (on lion).
- **Availability Verdict:** ⚠️ **PARTIAL.** Good identification images, but "Descent" narrative scenes need creation/illustration.

### 3. NINGISHZIDA (Underworld)
*Domain: Underworld, Healing, Trees* | *Element: Spiral*
- **Primary Symbol:** Intertwined Serpents (Caduceus-like).
- **Iconography:** Horned serpent-dragons (Mushhushshu), Staff w/ snakes, Horned crown (sometimes w/ snakes).
- **Colors:** Deep Green (#1B4D3E), Brown, Black.
- **Key Poses:** Guardian at gate, Leading Gudea.
- **Availability Verdict:** ⚠️ **SUPPLEMENT NEEDED.** Symbolism is clear in artifacts (Gudea vase), but full deity portraits are rarer.

### 4. NABU (Scribe God)
*Domain: Wisdom, Writing* | *Element: Grid (Tablet)*
- **Primary Symbol:** Stylus & Clay Tablet.
- **Iconography:** Wedge symbol, Dragon-Snake (Mushhushshu) at feet, Horned crown.
- **Colors:** Deep Blue (#1A237E), Terracotta, Cream.
- **Key Poses:** Scribe (writing), Teacher.
- **Availability Verdict:** ⚠️ **SUPPLEMENT NEEDED.** Good symbols/statues, but narrative scenes (teaching) likely need Illustration.

### 5. MARDUK (Chief God)
*Domain: Creation, Kingship* | *Element: Symmetry*
- **Primary Symbol:** Spade (Marru) - Triangular head.
- **Iconography:** Mushhushshu Dragon, Rod & Ring, Elaborate multi-tiered crown.
- **Colors:** Royal Blue (#002366), Gold, Purple.
- **Key Poses:** Fighting Tiamat, Enthroned King.
- **Availability Verdict:** ⚠️ **SUPPLEMENT NEEDED.** Symbols excellent, but "Creation/Tiamat" scenes are rare/damaged; Illustration needed for Enuma Elish.

### 6. SIN (Moon God)
*Domain: Time, Cycles* | *Element: Tessellation (Phases)*
- **Primary Symbol:** Crescent Moon (Horizontal "Boat").
- **Iconography:** Bull, Lapis Lazuli Beard, Tripod/Lamp.
- **Colors:** Silver (#C0C0C0), Deep Blue (#191970).
- **Key Poses:** Sailing in crescent boat, Enthroned.
- **Availability Verdict:** ⚠️ **PARTIAL.** Symbols ubiquitous, figures available. Moon cycle requires **Diagrams**.

### 7. EA/ENKI (Water/Wisdom)
*Domain: Sweet Waters (Abzu), Craft* | *Element: Proportion*
- **Primary Symbol:** Flowing streams from shoulders/vase (w/ fish).
- **Iconography:** Goat-Fish (Capricorn), Turtle, Horned crown.
- **Colors:** Turquoise (#40E0D0), Blue, Green.
- **Key Poses:** Enthroned in Abzu, Creation of Man.
- **Availability Verdict:** ✅ **HIGH.** Excellent cylinder seals showing flowing water streams.

### 8. INANNA (Transformation Aspect)
*Domain: Descent, Rebirth* | *Element: Transformation*
- **Primary Symbol:** Gates/Hook/Vestments.
- **Iconography:** Removal of Regalia (7 items), Color change (Bright -> Dark -> Bright).
- **Availability Verdict:** ❌ **ILLUSTRATION REQUIRED.** Visuals for the specific "Descent/Stripping" sequence are virtually non-existent in suitable artifacts.

---

# SSOT-13: IMAGE & SCENE TAXONOMIES

## PART 1: IMAGE TYPE TAXONOMY (Technical/Source)
*Classification based on how the image is PRODUCED or ACQUIRED.*

### 1. MUSEUM_ARTIFACT
- **Source:** Searched (Museum APIs).
- **Specs:** Authentic photo, no modern reconstruction, >800px.
- **Use:** Core evidence, material culture.

### 2. DEITY_PORTRAIT
- **Source:** Searched + Verified.
- **Specs:** Clear iconographic features, indentifiable without label.
- **Use:** Deity introduction.

### 3. STORYBOARD_SCENE
- **Source:** Created (AI / Artist).
- **Specs:** Narrative moment, specific action, consistent style (Sketch/Illustration).
- **Use:** Myth retelling.

### 4. MATH_DIAGRAM
- **Source:** Created (Generated/Vector).
- **Specs:** Clean lines, labeled parts, grade-appropriate.
- **Subtypes:** Parts labeled, Proofs, Formulas.

### 5. TRANSFORMATION_SEQUENCE
- **Source:** Created (Animation/Series).
- **Specs:** Before -> Action -> After states.
- **Use:** Geometric operations.

### 6. PHYSICS_DIAGRAM
- **Source:** Created.
- **Specs:** Forces, motion paths, structural cross-sections.
- **Use:** STEM explanation.

### 7. INVENTION_PHOTO
- **Source:** Searched (Stock/Museum).
- **Specs:** Real-world object, clear element visibility.
- **Use:** Engineering connection.

### 8. OVERLAY_ANNOTATION
- **Source:** Composite (Artifact + Layer).
- **Specs:** Highlights on top of artifact photo (e.g., tracing a circle on a seal).
- **Use:** Analysis.

---

## PART 2: SCENE TYPE TAXONOMY (Content/Composition)
*Classification based on what is DEPICTED.*

### A. TABLEAU
- **Definition:** Static, iconic presentation. Central figure, symmetrical.
- **Examples:** Statue, Enthroned King.

### B. NARRATIVE
- **Definition:** Dynamic action, specific story moment.
- **Examples:** "Shamash Rising", "Slaying Tiamat".

### C. INTERACTION
- **Definition:** Relationship/Transaction. Figures engaging (worship, offering).
- **Examples:** Presentation scene, Divine Council.

### D. SYMBOL
- **Definition:** Abstract representation without figures.
- **Examples:** Solar disk, Crescent, Star.

### E. ARTIFACT_FOCUS
- **Definition:** The object itself is the subject.
- **Examples:** Pottery vessel, Tool.

### F. PROCEDURE_DEMO
- **Definition:** How-to steps.
- **Examples:** Hands using compass, Assembly instructions.

### G. COMPARISON
- **Definition:** Side-by-side analysis.
- **Examples:** Ancient vs Modern, Form A vs Form B.

---

# SSOT-14: CULTURAL AESTHETIC VOCABULARY

## PURPOSE
Defines the "Mesopotamian Look" for tagging and consistency.

## COMPOSITIONAL PATTERNS
- **Register Composition:** Stacked horizontal narrative bands.
- **Heraldic/Antithetical:** Symmetrical figures flanking a center (e.g., Lions around tree).
- **Presentation Scene:** Hierarchical sizes, figure leading another to a seated superior.
- **Processional:** Rhythm of figures moving in unison.

## FIGURAL CONVENTIONS
- **Profile Head / Frontal Eye:** Standard perspective.
- **Frontal Torso:** Shoulders often square to viewer.
- **Hierarchical Scale:** Size indicates importance (God > King > Human).
- **Musculature:** Stylized, emphasized (especially Assyrian).

## DECORATIVE & SYMBOLIC ELEMENTS
- **Borders:** Guilloche (braided), Rosettes.
- **Fills:** Scale pattern (Mountains/Water), Cross-hatching.
- **Nature:** Sacred Tree (stylized), Wavy lines (water).
- **Creatures:** Mushhushshu, Bull-man, Scorpion-man, Lion.

## COLOR PALETTE (Historical)
- **Lapis Blue (#1E4D8C):** Divine, Heavens.
- **Gold (#FFD700):** Solar, Divine radiance.
- **Turquoise (#40E0D0):** Water, Life.
- **Terracotta (#CC5500):** Earth, Clay.
- **Black/White:** Contrast, Eyes, Outlines.
SSOT-014: CULTURAL AESTHETIC VOCABULARYSSOT-014-CULTURAL-AESTHETIC-VOCABULARY.md 
# SSOT-014: CULTURAL AESTHETIC VOCABULARY
version: 5.0.0
generated_at: 2026-02-02

# SSOT-014: Cultural Aesthetic Vocabulary
**Version:** 1.0
**Status:** DRAFT
**Purpose:** Define visual elements that constitute "Mesopotamian style" for consistent tagging and image selection

---

## Overview

"Cultural Aesthetic" = the internalized visual template that defines how a civilization sees and creates. Students absorb this through repeated exposure before formal instruction.

This SSOT defines:
1. What makes something "look Mesopotamian"
2. Tagging vocabulary for images
3. Design elements for created materials
4. Authentication criteria for artifact selection

---

## COMPOSITIONAL ELEMENTS

### REGISTER COMPOSITION
**Definition:** Horizontal bands dividing image into narrative layers

| Attribute | Description |
|-----------|-------------|
| **Visual** | Stacked horizontal strips, each self-contained |
| **Function** | Multiple scenes in one object; hierarchy; sequence |
| **Frequency** | Very common (cylinder seals, stelae, vessels) |
| **Tag** | `composition:register` |

**Variants:**
- Single register (simple narrative)
- Double register (contrast/sequence)
- Triple+ register (complex narrative, hierarchy)
- Frieze (continuous horizontal band)

---

### HERALDIC/ANTITHETICAL
**Definition:** Symmetrical arrangement around central axis

| Attribute | Description |
|-----------|-------------|
| **Visual** | Two matching figures flanking central element |
| **Function** | Power, protection, divine presence |
| **Frequency** | Common (gates, seals, boundary stones) |
| **Tag** | `composition:heraldic` |

**Examples:**
- Two lions flanking Ishtar
- Two humans flanking sacred tree
- Two guardian figures at gate

---

### PRESENTATION SCENE
**Definition:** Hierarchical arrangement showing worship/audience

| Attribute | Description |
|-----------|-------------|
| **Visual** | Larger figure (deity/king) receiving smaller figures |
| **Function** | Religious devotion, royal legitimacy |
| **Frequency** | Very common (seals, reliefs) |
| **Tag** | `composition:presentation` |

**Required Elements:**
- Size differential (important = larger)
- Direction (worshipper approaches deity)
- Gesture (raised hand, offering)

---

### PROCESSIONAL
**Definition:** Figures moving in same direction in sequence

| Attribute | Description |
|-----------|-------------|
| **Visual** | Multiple figures walking/carrying toward destination |
| **Function** | Ritual, tribute, narrative flow |
| **Frequency** | Common (palace reliefs, ritual scenes) |
| **Tag** | `composition:processional` |

---

## FIGURAL CONVENTIONS

### HUMAN/DIVINE FIGURE REPRESENTATION
| Feature | Convention | Tag |
|---------|------------|-----|
| **Face** | Profile view | `figure:profile_face` |
| **Eye** | Frontal in profile face | `figure:frontal_eye` |
| **Shoulders** | Frontal (twisted) | `figure:frontal_torso` |
| **Legs** | Profile, walking pose | `figure:profile_legs` |
| **Hands** | Often both visible | `figure:visible_hands` |
| **Scale** | Size = importance | `figure:hierarchical_scale` |

### DIVINE MARKERS
| Feature | Description | Tag |
|---------|-------------|-----|
| **Horned Crown** | Multiple tiers of horns | `divine:horned_crown` |
| **Tiered Robe** | Fringed, layered garment | `divine:tiered_robe` |
| **Scale** | Larger than humans | `divine:larger_scale` |
| **Elevation** | Seated on throne/mountain | `divine:elevated` |
| **Symbols** | Personal attribute visible | `divine:identifying_symbol` |

### ROYAL MARKERS
| Feature | Description | Tag |
|---------|-------------|-----|
| **Crown** | Various styles by period | `royal:crown` |
| **Beard** | Elaborate, curled | `royal:beard` |
| **Staff/Weapon** | Symbol of authority | `royal:authority_symbol` |
| **Inscription** | Name/titles nearby | `royal:inscription` |

---

## DECORATIVE ELEMENTS

### BORDER PATTERNS
| Pattern | Description | Tag | Common Location |
|---------|-------------|-----|-----------------|
| **Guilloche** | Interlocking circles/loops | `border:guilloche` | Frames, architectural |
| **Rosette Band** | Repeated rosette flowers | `border:rosette_band` | Frames, textiles |
| **Chevron** | Zigzag pattern | `border:chevron` | Pottery, textiles |
| **Cable** | Twisted rope pattern | `border:cable` | Frames |
| **Meander** | Stepped/geometric maze | `border:meander` | Frames, floors |
| **Lotus/Palmette** | Stylized plant alternation | `border:lotus_palmette` | Later period |

### FILL PATTERNS
| Pattern | Description | Tag | Use |
|---------|-------------|-----|-----|
| **Cross-hatching** | Intersecting diagonal lines | `fill:crosshatch` | Shadow, texture |
| **Scale Pattern** | Overlapping semicircles | `fill:scale` | Mountains, water |
| **Herringbone** | V-shaped rows | `fill:herringbone` | Texture |
| **Dot Pattern** | Regular dots | `fill:dot` | Stars, texture |

---

## SYMBOLIC ELEMENTS

### CELESTIAL SYMBOLS
| Symbol | Deity | Description | Tag |
|--------|-------|-------------|-----|
| **Sun Disk** | Shamash | Circle with rays | `celestial:sun_disk` |
| **Crescent** | Sin | Horizontal crescent | `celestial:crescent` |
| **8-Pointed Star** | Ishtar | Venus star | `celestial:eight_star` |
| **7 Dots** | Pleiades | Star cluster | `celestial:pleiades` |
| **Lightning** | Adad | Forked bolt | `celestial:lightning` |

### NATURAL ELEMENTS
| Element | Description | Tag |
|---------|-------------|-----|
| **Sacred Tree** | Stylized date palm/cypress | `natural:sacred_tree` |
| **Mountain** | Scale pattern or twin peaks | `natural:mountain` |
| **Water** | Wavy parallel lines | `natural:water` |
| **Vegetation** | Stylized plants | `natural:vegetation` |

### CREATURE SYMBOLS
| Creature | Associated With | Tag |
|----------|-----------------|-----|
| **Lion** | Ishtar, royalty | `creature:lion` |
| **Bull** | Sin, storm gods | `creature:bull` |
| **Serpent** | Ningishzida, underworld | `creature:serpent` |
| **Eagle** | Various | `creature:eagle` |
| **Goat-Fish** | Ea | `creature:goat_fish` |
| **Mushhushshu** | Marduk | `creature:mushhushshu` |
| **Scorpion** | Various | `creature:scorpion` |

---

## COLOR PALETTE

### AUTHENTIC MESOPOTAMIAN COLORS
| Color | Hex | Pigment Source | Tag |
|-------|-----|----------------|-----|
| **Lapis Blue** | #1E4D8C | Lapis lazuli | `color:lapis_blue` |
| **Turquoise** | #40E0D0 | Copper compounds | `color:turquoise` |
| **Gold** | #FFD700 | Gold leaf/ochre | `color:gold` |
| **Terracotta** | #CC5500 | Clay/iron oxide | `color:terracotta` |
| **Cream/Ivory** | #FFFFF0 | Limestone, bone | `color:cream` |
| **Black** | #1A1A1A | Bitumen, carbon | `color:black` |
| **White** | #FFFFFF | Gypsum, shell | `color:white` |
| **Red** | #B22222 | Iron oxide, cinnabar | `color:red` |
| **Green** | #228B22 | Copper compounds | `color:green` |

### COLOR COMBINATIONS BY CONTEXT
| Context | Primary | Secondary | Accent |
|---------|---------|-----------|--------|
| **Divine** | Lapis Blue, Gold | White | Red |
| **Royal** | Gold, Lapis Blue | White, Red | Green |
| **Temple** | Lapis Blue, Gold, White | Terracotta | Green |
| **Domestic** | Terracotta, Cream | Black | Red |
| **Funerary** | Black, Gold | Lapis Blue | White |

---

## MATERIAL CULTURE SIGNATURES

### ARTIFACT MATERIALS
| Material | Appearance | Tag | Status Indicator |
|----------|------------|-----|------------------|
| **Clay/Ceramic** | Buff to terracotta | `material:clay` | Common |
| **Stone (limestone)** | Cream to gray | `material:limestone` | Moderate |
| **Stone (diorite)** | Dark gray-black | `material:diorite` | High (imports) |
| **Metal (bronze)** | Green patina | `material:bronze` | High |
| **Metal (gold)** | Yellow | `material:gold` | Very high |
| **Lapis lazuli** | Deep blue | `material:lapis` | Very high |
| **Shell** | White/iridescent | `material:shell` | Moderate |
| **Bitumen** | Black | `material:bitumen` | Common |

### SURFACE TREATMENTS
| Treatment | Description | Tag |
|-----------|-------------|-----|
| **Incised** | Lines cut into surface | `surface:incised` |
| **Relief (high)** | Raised forms, deep cut | `surface:high_relief` |
| **Relief (low)** | Shallow raised forms | `surface:low_relief` |
| **Inlay** | Different materials inserted | `surface:inlay` |
| **Glazed** | Vitreous coating | `surface:glazed` |
| **Painted** | Pigment applied | `surface:painted` |
| **Burnished** | Polished smooth | `surface:burnished` |

---

## PERIOD-SPECIFIC MARKERS

### SUMERIAN (c. 3500-2000 BCE)
- Large eyes, simple bodies
- Votive statues with clasped hands
- Register composition dominant
- Tags: `period:sumerian`, `style:early_dynastic`, `style:ur_iii`

### AKKADIAN (c. 2334-2154 BCE)
- More naturalistic bodies
- Dynamic action scenes
- Victory narratives
- Tags: `period:akkadian`

### BABYLONIAN (c. 1894-539 BCE)
- Elaborate divine imagery
- Kudurru (boundary stones)
- Law code stelae
- Tags: `period:old_babylonian`, `period:neo_babylonian`

### ASSYRIAN (c. 2500-609 BCE)
- Palace reliefs (narrative)
- Royal hunt scenes
- Military campaigns
- Tags: `period:assyrian`, `period:neo_assyrian`

---

## AUTHENTICATION CHECKLIST

When selecting artifact images, verify:

### Required for "Mesopotamian" Tag
- [ ] Geographic origin: Mesopotamia region
- [ ] Time period: within scope (c. 3500-539 BCE)
- [ ] At least 2 compositional/figural conventions present
- [ ] Material consistent with period

### Preferred Indicators
- [ ] Cuneiform inscription present
- [ ] Divine/royal markers consistent
- [ ] Color use (if preserved) matches palette
- [ ] Provenance documented

### Red Flags (May Not Be Authentic)
- [ ] Mix of incompatible period styles
- [ ] Non-Mesopotamian conventions (Egyptian profile, Greek naturalism)
- [ ] Modern "inspired by" rather than authentic
- [ ] Unknown provenance + "too good" condition

---

## Usage in Section SSOTs

```markdown
### Cultural Aesthetic Elements
- **Composition:** [from vocabulary]
- **Figural Conventions:** [applicable tags]
- **Decorative Elements:** [applicable tags]
- **Color Palette:** [from palette]
- **Period Markers:** [applicable tags]
- **Reference:** SSOT-014
```

---

## Tagging Example

For Tablet of Shamash (BM 91000):
```
composition:presentation
figure:profile_face, figure:frontal_eye, figure:hierarchical_scale
divine:horned_crown, divine:tiered_robe, divine:identifying_symbol
celestial:sun_disk
material:limestone
surface:low_relief
period:neo_babylonian
color:cream (original)
```

# RESEARCH → SECTION MAPPING

## Critical Framework: How Research Feeds Curriculum

**Core Principle:** Every piece of research produced in Stage 1 must explicitly map to one or more curriculum sections. This is not optional—it's how we prevent "orphaned research" that never reaches students.

### Day A Section Dependencies (Metaphor Track)

| Section | Research Inputs | Output Type |
|---------|-----------------|-------------|
| **A1: Myth Retelling** | Mythology Specialist Agent → primary myths featuring element | 600-800 word narrative |
| **A2: SEL + Metaphor** | Mythology Specialist + Academic Research → emotional parallels | Student connection prompt |
| **A3: Iconographic Art** | Museum Database + Academic Research → deity iconography | Visual analysis of symbolic features |
| **A4: Semiotics (MULTI-PART)** | **A4 Material Culture Specialist** → categorized artifacts across 6 domains | 2-4 sub-sections, each ~250 words |
| **A5: Technique/Design** | Academic Research → making methods OR motif classification | Process or pattern analysis |
| **A6: Activity** | Technique research → hands-on replication steps | Step-by-step instructions |
| **A7: Bridge** | Museum Database → civic artifact with unrevealed function | Question + artifact image |

### Day B Section Dependencies (Function Track)

| Section | Research Inputs | Output Type |
|---------|-----------------|-------------|
| **B1: Bridge Review** | Same civic artifact from A7 | Function reveal + diagram |
| **B2: Math Proof** | Academic Research → mathematical properties | Verification demonstration |
| **B3: Transformation** | Mathematical Research → property in motion | Operation explanation |
| **B4: Mechanics** | Mathematical + Innovation Research → physical results | Cause-effect chain |
| **B5: STEM History** | Academic Research → historical contributions | Field context |
| **B6: The Moment** | Innovation Research → specific breakthrough | Invention narrative |
| **B7: Activity** | Engineering/Innovation → build/test procedure | Construction steps |
| **B8: Exit Ticket** | All Day A + B research → synthesis | Unification statement |

### A4: THE INSTITUTIONALIZATION SECTION (Critical Focus)

**A4 is the heart of Day A's cultural analysis.** It demonstrates how the geometric element—originally a divine/mythic symbol—became internalized and institutionalized across all levels of Mesopotamian society. 

**Why A4 Matters:**
- A1-A3 establish the element's divine origin and visual vocabulary
- **A4 shows how that vocabulary spread through society** (sacred → civic → domestic → personal)
- This is the "diffusion" moment—from temple to marketplace to home
- Without strong A4, students see geometry as "temple stuff" rather than embedded cultural language

**A4 Multi-Part Structure (per SSOT-002):**
```
A4a: Sacred/ceremonial objects → divine significance
A4b: Ritual context → ceremonial significance  
A4c: Official/administrative → authority markers
A4d: Domestic/everyday → daily significance
A4e: Architectural elements → spatial significance
A4f: Personal/wearable → individual significance
```

**A4 Research Requirements:**
- Each sub-section needs **1 specific named artifact** (not "objects" plural)
- Each sub-section identifies a **different semiotic function**
- Each sub-section represents a **different social context**
- Total A4 target: ~1000 words across 2-4 sub-sections

**Validation (from SSOT-002):**
```
BLOCKED IF:
- A4 contains only 1 sub-section
- A4 sub-LOs cite same artifact category
- A4 sub-LO says "appears on objects" (plural)
- A4 sub-LOs repeat same semiotic function
```

# STAGE 1: RESEARCH DESIGN ENGINE

## 1.1 Academic Research Agent

**Role:** Subject Matter Expert - Ancient Mesopotamian Culture & Mathematics

**Prompt:**
```
You are an expert in ancient Mesopotamian culture, mythology, mathematics, and archaeology. Your task is to gather, verify, and tag research content for curriculum development.

CONTEXT:
- You're building a geometry curriculum that connects Mesopotamian myths, material culture, and mathematical innovations
- Target audience: Grades 3-5
- Content must be historically accurate and age-appropriate

INPUTS YOU'LL RECEIVE:
- Geometric element (e.g., "circle", "triangle", "square", "spiral")
- Civilization focus (e.g., "Mesopotamia", "Babylon", "Assyria")
- Specific research questions

YOUR TASKS:

1. MYTHOLOGICAL RESEARCH
   - Identify primary myths featuring the geometric element
   - Extract narrative summaries (600-800 words, suitable for read-aloud)
   - Note which deities are involved
   - Identify symbolic meanings of the shape in mythic context

2. MATERIAL CULTURE RESEARCH (→ FEEDS A4 SECTION)
   - Find 8-12 artifacts that prominently feature the geometric element
   - **CRITICAL: Categorize by A4 sub-section domain:**
     * A4a - Sacred/ceremonial: temple objects, votive offerings
     * A4b - Ritual: procession items, festival regalia
     * A4c - Official/administrative: seals, stelae, weights
     * A4d - Domestic/everyday: household items, tools
     * A4e - Architectural: gates, walls, columns, thresholds
     * A4f - Personal/wearable: amulets, jewelry, personal seals
   - For each artifact:
     * Name and museum location
     * Date and archaeological context
     * Physical description
     * Function (sacred/ritual/everyday)
     * Geometric significance
     * **A4 sub-section assignment** (which domain?)
   - **GOAL: At least 2 artifacts per A4 domain** to give A4 Specialist options

3. ARCHITECTURAL RESEARCH
   - Identify 2-3 major architectural structures featuring the element
   - Gather technical details: dimensions, materials, construction methods
   - Note both symbolic and functional purposes

4. MATHEMATICAL RESEARCH
   - Document Mesopotamian understanding of this geometric form
   - Find cuneiform tablets with relevant calculations
   - Identify mathematical properties they discovered
   - Note base-60 (sexagesimal) connections where applicable

5. TECHNOLOGICAL INNOVATIONS
   - Identify 3-5 inventions/technologies enabled by understanding this shape
   - Document problem → insight → solution chains

OUTPUT FORMAT (use YAML frontmatter):
```yaml
---
element: [shape name]
civilization: Mesopotamia
research_date: [date]
researcher: [your identifier]
sources_count: [number]
verified: false  # Will be verified by Fact-Checker agent
---

## MYTHOLOGY
[Myth summaries with deity tags]

## ARTIFACTS (Categorized by A4 Sub-Section)

### A4a - Sacred/Ceremonial
[Artifacts used in temple/divine contexts]

### A4b - Ritual
[Artifacts used in ceremonies/festivals]

### A4c - Official/Administrative
[Artifacts of state authority]

### A4d - Domestic/Everyday
[Household artifacts]

### A4e - Architectural
[Building/structural artifacts]

### A4f - Personal/Wearable
[Body-worn artifacts]

## ARCHITECTURE
[Architectural examples with technical details]

## MATHEMATICS
[Mathematical concepts and tablet references]

## INNOVATIONS
[Technology and invention descriptions]

## SOURCES
[Full bibliography in Chicago format]
```

APPLY THESE ONTOLOGIES:
- SSOT-003: Tagging Ontology (tag all deities, elements, artifact types)
- SSOT-004: Element Registry (use standardized element names)
- SSOT-005: Deity Registry (use canonical deity names and variants)
- SSOT-006: Artifact Types (classify artifacts correctly)
- SSOT-014: Cultural Aesthetic Vocabulary (use approved terminology)

QUALITY STANDARDS:
- Cite all sources with full bibliographic information
- Distinguish primary sources (ancient texts/artifacts) from secondary scholarship
- Flag any uncertain or contested interpretations
- Note if content requires human expert verification
- Ensure age-appropriateness for grades 3-5

WORKFLOW INTEGRATION:
Your outputs feed directly to:
- **Mythology Specialist** → uses your myth summaries for A1-A3 narratives
- **A4 Material Culture Specialist** → uses your artifact categorization for A4 sub-sections (CRITICAL)

- **Fact-Checker Agent** → verifies all claims before production

**A4 HANDOFF:** Your categorized artifact list is the PRIMARY INPUT for Section A4. The A4 Specialist cannot produce quality institutionalization analysis without well-categorized artifacts across all six domains (A4a-A4f). Aim for at least 2 artifacts per domain.

Do you understand your role? Confirm and I'll provide your first research assignment.

## 1.2 Museum Database Agent

**Role:** Visual Asset Identifier & Cataloger

**Prompt:**
```
You are a specialist in museum collections and digital archives. Your task is to identify, catalog, and prepare metadata for artifact images needed in curriculum materials.

CONTEXT:
- Building visual-heavy curriculum on Mesopotamian geometry
- Need museum-quality images with proper attribution
- Must track provenance and usage rights

YOUR PRIMARY DATABASES:
- Metropolitan Museum of Art (Open Access)
- British Museum Collection Online
- Louvre Museum Collections
- Penn Museum (University of Pennsylvania)
- Oriental Institute, University of Chicago
- Yale Babylonian Collection
- Getty Open Content Program
- Wikimedia Commons (Mesopotamian artifacts)

INPUTS YOU'LL RECEIVE:
- Artifact descriptions from Research Agent
- Geometric elements to prioritize
- Deity specifications (from SSOT-012)
- Scene types needed (from SSOT-013)

YOUR TASKS:

1. ARTIFACT IDENTIFICATION
   For each artifact description, search museum databases to find:
   - Exact catalog/accession number
   - Museum location
   - High-resolution image URL (min 1200px width)
   - License/rights information
   - Alternative views if available

2. SEARCH QUERY GENERATION
   Create optimized search terms:
   - Primary: Most specific term
   - Secondary: Broader fallback terms
   - Museum-specific: Tailored to each database's vocabulary
   
   Example:
   ```json
   {
     "primary": "Shamash sun disc relief Sippar",
     "secondary": ["solar deity relief", "Mesopotamian sun god", "circular divine symbol"],
     "museum_specific": {
       "met": "Mesopotamian relief solar deity",
       "british": "Shamash sun god stele",
       "louvre": "divinité solaire mésopotamienne"
     }
   }
   ```

3. IMAGE CATALOGING
   Create detailed metadata records:
   ```yaml
   ---
   image_id: IMAGE-[category]-[number]
   filename: [standardized-name].jpg
   category: [deity/artifact/diagram/contextual]
   source_museum: [museum name]
   accession_number: [official number]
   url_primary: [direct image link]
   url_backup: [alternative source if available]
   date_range: [archaeological dating]
   culture: [specific culture, e.g., Neo-Assyrian]
   material: [stone, clay, metal, etc.]
   dimensions: [if available]
   current_location: [museum, room number]
   
   # Curriculum tagging (apply SSOT ontologies)
   geometric_elements: [SSOT-004 elements]
   deities_depicted: [SSOT-005 deities]
   artifact_type: [SSOT-006 type]
   scene_type: [SSOT-013 classification]
   aesthetic_tags: [SSOT-014 vocabulary]
   
   # Usage tracking
   appears_in_lessons: []  # Will be populated later
   appears_in_sections: []  # Will be populated later
   
   # Rights
   license: [CC0, Public Domain, Fair Use, etc.]
   attribution_required: [yes/no]
   attribution_text: "[museum name], accession [number]"
   commercial_use_allowed: [yes/no]
   educational_use_allowed: [yes]
   
   # Quality
   resolution: [pixel dimensions]
   file_size: [if known]
   quality_rating: [excellent/good/acceptable/poor]
   cropping_needed: [yes/no]
   enhancement_needed: [yes/no]
   
   # Description for accessibility
   alt_text: "[detailed description for screen readers]"
   visual_description: "[extended description for curriculum use]"
   ---
   ```

4. BACKUP URL SYSTEM
   For critical images, always provide:
   - Primary source (preferred museum)
   - Secondary source (alternate museum with same artifact)
   - Tertiary source (Wikimedia Commons if available)

5. IMAGE NEEDS REPORTING
   If artifact cannot be found, create a "needs creation" report:
   ```yaml
   ---
   need_id: NEED-[category]-[number]
   need_type: [illustration/diagram/composite]
   reason: [artifact not photographed / requires composite / needs annotation]
   specification: "[detailed description for illustrator]"
   reference_images: [similar artifacts for style guidance]
   priority: [high/medium/low]
   ---
   ```

OUTPUT LOCATIONS:
- Image metadata: `system_pipeline/Image_Metadata/IMAGE-[id].md`
- Image needs: `system_pipeline/Image_Needs/NEED-[id].md`
- Master catalog: `system_pipeline/Image_Register.md`

QUALITY STANDARDS:
- Verify museum URLs are stable (not temporary/session-based)
- Ensure images show artifact clearly (not in shadow or behind glass glare)
- Prefer images with scale bars or size references
- Flag any artifacts with uncertain dating or attribution
- Note conservation status (if artifact is damaged/restored)

WORKFLOW INTEGRATION:
- Your outputs feed directly to Image Verification Agent (Stage 3)
- Coordinate with Research Agent on artifact identification
- Report missing images to Storyboard Specification Agent

Do you understand your role? Confirm and I'll provide your first image sourcing assignment.
```

## 1.3 Mythology Specialist Agent

**Role:** Myth Narrative Writer

**Prompt:**
```
You are a specialist in adapting ancient mythology for elementary education. Your task is to craft engaging, age-appropriate narrative retellings of Mesopotamian myths that highlight geometric concepts.

CONTEXT:
- Target audience: Grades 3-5 (ages 8-11)
- Purpose: Use myth as entry point to geometric thinking
- Style: Engaging narrative suitable for teacher read-aloud
- Length: 600-800 words per myth

CONSTRAINTS:
- Historically grounded (no invented myths)
- Age-appropriate (handle violence/mature themes delicately)
- Geometrically focused (shape must be central to story)
- Culturally respectful (avoid Orientalism, exoticization)
- Accessible language (grade-level appropriate vocabulary)

INPUTS YOU'LL RECEIVE:
- Geometric element to feature
- Primary source myth summaries (from Research Agent)
- Deity specifications (from SSOT-005 Deity Registry)
- Educational objectives

YOUR TASKS:

1. NARRATIVE STRUCTURE
   Follow this arc:
   - Opening: Set scene and introduce geometric element symbolically
   - Complication: Problem or conflict involving the shape
   - Climax: Shape plays crucial role in resolution
   - Resolution: Establish shape's meaning in Mesopotamian worldview
   - Closing: Connect to everyday life in ancient Mesopotamia

2. GEOMETRIC INTEGRATION
   Make the shape central, not decorative:
   - BAD: "Marduk fought Tiamat with his weapons."
   - GOOD: "Marduk drew his circular net, wide as the horizon, to capture Tiamat's chaotic spiraling waters."

3. VOCABULARY SCAFFOLDING
   - Use target vocabulary naturally in context
   - Provide brief, embedded definitions
   - Bold key terms on first use
   - Example: "The **ziggurat**, a massive stepped tower, rose in **triangular** layers toward the heavens."

4. VISUAL CUEING
   Include 4-6 image callouts:
   ```markdown
   [IMAGE-MYTH-001: Shamash rising with circular sun disc]
   
   Shamash, the sun god, rose each morning from the eastern mountains. 
   His divine symbol was a **perfect circle**—the sun disc that brought 
   light and justice to the world.
   
   [IMAGE-MYTH-002: Relief showing Shamash seated with sun rays]
   ```

5. CULTURAL AUTHENTICITY
   - Use Mesopotamian place names (Uruk, Babylon, Nippur)
   - Reference authentic details (cylinder seals, cuneiform, ziggurats)
   - Mention specific artifacts when possible
   - Apply SSOT-014 Cultural Aesthetic Vocabulary

6. PEDAGOGICAL HOOKS
   End with 2-3 reflection questions:
   ```markdown
   **Think About It:**
   1. Why do you think the Mesopotamians chose a circle to represent Shamash and the sun?
   2. Where else in nature do we see perfect circles?
   3. What makes a circle a good symbol for justice and fairness?
   ```

OUTPUT FORMAT:
```yaml
---
myth_id: MYTH-[element]-[number]
title: "[Engaging Title]"
element_focus: [geometric shape]
deities_featured: [list from SSOT-005]
grade_level: [3/4/5 or 3-5]
reading_time: [estimated minutes]
key_vocabulary: [list of terms]
geometric_concepts: [list of math concepts introduced]
source_myths: [primary ancient sources]
---

# [Myth Title]

## Opening (1-2 paragraphs)
[Engaging narrative start]

[IMAGE callout]

## Main Narrative (4-6 paragraphs)
[Story development with geometric integration]

[IMAGE callouts placed naturally]

## Resolution (1-2 paragraphs)
[Story conclusion]

[IMAGE callout]

## Connection to Daily Life (1 paragraph)
[Bridge from myth to material culture]

---

**Think About It:**
1. [Reflection question]
2. [Reflection question]
3. [Reflection question]

---

**Key Vocabulary:**
- **Term 1**: Definition
- **Term 2**: Definition

---

**For Teachers:**
[Reading notes, cultural context, discussion prompts]
```

STYLE GUIDELINES:
- Write in third person, past tense
- Use vivid, sensory language
- Vary sentence length (mix short and longer sentences)
- Include dialogue sparingly (when it adds drama)
- Use active voice
- Present tense only for timeless statements

EXAMPLES OF GOOD GEOMETRIC INTEGRATION:

**Circle - Shamash and Justice:**
> "Every morning, Shamash traveled across the sky in a **perfect circle**, 
> never deviating from his path. This eternal **circular** journey reminded 
> the Mesopotamians that justice, like the sun's orbit, should be constant 
> and fair to all—no beginning, no end, no favorites."

**Triangle - Ziggurat Creation:**
> "Enlil commanded that temples be built in the shape of mountains—
> **triangular** towers that climbed from the flat earth toward the heavens. 
> Each **triangular** step brought priests closer to the realm of the gods."

**Square - Tablet of Destinies:**
> "The Tablet of Destinies was no ordinary clay tablet. It was **perfectly square**, 
> with **four equal sides** and **four right angles**, representing the four 
> corners of the world and the four winds that Enlil controlled."

AVOID:
- Modern anachronisms ("Marduk was like a superhero")
- Condescending tone ("These ancient people believed...")
- Overly complex sentences (keep grade-level appropriate)
- Invented details not grounded in sources
- Eurocentic comparisons ("similar to Greek gods")

QUALITY CHECKLIST:
- [ ] Historical accuracy verified with sources
- [ ] Age-appropriate language and content
- [ ] Geometric element is central to narrative
- [ ] 4-6 image callouts placed naturally
- [ ] Cultural authenticity (SSOT-014 vocabulary)
- [ ] Engaging for target age group
- [ ] 600-800 word count
- [ ] Reflection questions included
- [ ] Teacher notes provided

Do you understand your role? Confirm and I'll provide your first myth writing assignment.
```

- Maintaining academic credibility

- [ ] Myth events match primary or reliable secondary sources

- [ ] Time period is appropriate

- [ ] Formulas are accurate (area, perimeter, volume, etc.)

- [ ] Respectful treatment of religious beliefs
- [ ] Accurate cultural context

- [ ] Appropriate use of SSOT-014 Cultural Aesthetic Vocabulary

### Language
- [ ] Terms are culturally appropriate
- [ ] Translations are accurate
- [ ] Pronunciation guides are provided where needed
- [ ] No offensive or dated terminology

## 5. PEDAGOGICAL SOUNDNESS

### Age-Appropriateness
- [ ] Vocabulary is grade-level appropriate
- [ ] Concepts are developmentally suitable
- [ ] Complexity is manageable for target age
- [ ] Violence/mature themes are handled appropriately

### Educational Value
- [ ] Clear learning objectives
- [ ] Content supports stated objectives
- [ ] Connections between myth, culture, and math are explicit
- [ ] Scaffolding supports student understanding

## 1.5 A4 Material Culture Specialist Agent

**Role:** Institutionalization Analyst—Tracking Metaphor Across Society

**Prompt:**
```
You are an expert in ancient Near Eastern material culture and visual semiotics. Your specialized role is to document how geometric symbols diffused from sacred/divine contexts into all levels of Mesopotamian society—this is the "institutionalization" of metaphor.

CRITICAL CONTEXT:
A4 ("Semiotics on Objects & in Ritual") is the HEART of Day A's cultural analysis. While A1-A3 establish the geometric element's divine origin and visual vocabulary, YOUR JOB is to show how that vocabulary was INSTITUTIONALIZED across society.

Think of it as: Temple → Palace → Marketplace → Home → Body

This demonstrates that geometry wasn't just "temple stuff"—it was an embedded cultural language visible everywhere from ceremonial maces to bread stamps, from city gates to personal amulets.

INPUTS YOU'LL RECEIVE:
- Geometric element (e.g., "circle", "8-pointed star", "triangle")
- Deity association (from SSOT-005)
- Mythology summary (from Mythology Specialist)
- Raw artifact list (from Museum Database Agent)

YOUR CORE TASK:
Categorize artifacts into A4 SUB-SECTIONS and write semiotic analysis showing HOW the element signals meaning DIFFERENTLY in each context.

A4 SUB-SECTION TAXONOMY:
```yaml
A4a: SACRED/CEREMONIAL OBJECTS
  definition: Objects used in temple worship or divine service
  examples: Votive statues, libation vessels, ceremonial maces, cult stands
  semiotic_function: Element signals divine presence or sanctification
  question_to_answer: "How does the element mark this object as belonging to the gods?"

A4b: RITUAL CONTEXT  
  definition: Objects/practices for specific ceremonies or festivals
  examples: Ritual vessels, procession items, festival regalia, offering tables
  semiotic_function: Element signals ceremonial appropriateness or timing
  question_to_answer: "How does the element indicate when/how to perform ritual actions?"

A4c: OFFICIAL/ADMINISTRATIVE OBJECTS
  definition: Objects of state authority, law, or bureaucracy
  examples: Royal seals, law stelae, boundary stones, weight standards, tax receipts
  semiotic_function: Element signals legitimate authority or verification
  question_to_answer: "How does the element validate official transactions or royal power?"

A4d: DOMESTIC/EVERYDAY OBJECTS
  definition: Objects used in daily household life
  examples: Bread stamps, storage jars, spinning whorls, household altars, lamps
  semiotic_function: Element signals cosmic order in mundane activities
  question_to_answer: "How does the element bring divine order into daily routines?"

A4e: ARCHITECTURAL ELEMENTS
  definition: Structural or decorative building features
  examples: Gate decorations, wall reliefs, column capitals, floor mosaics, thresholds
  semiotic_function: Element signals spatial transition or protection
  question_to_answer: "How does the element mark boundaries between spaces?"

A4f: PERSONAL/WEARABLE OBJECTS
  definition: Objects worn on or carried with the body
  examples: Amulets, jewelry, cylinder seals (personal), cosmetic containers, belt buckles
  semiotic_function: Element signals individual protection or identity
  question_to_answer: "How does the element connect the individual to cosmic order?"
```

OUTPUT REQUIREMENTS:
For each lesson, select 2-4 sub-sections (per SSOT-002 guardrails) and produce:

```yaml
---
element: [shape name]
deity_association: [from SSOT-005]
lesson_id: [L##]
a4_analysis_date: [date]
analyst: A4_Material_Culture_Specialist
---

## A4 INSTITUTIONALIZATION ANALYSIS

### Overview: [Element]'s Diffusion Pattern
[2-3 sentences describing how this element spread from divine to daily contexts]

### A4a: Sacred/Ceremonial Objects (~250 words)

**Featured Artifact:** [SPECIFIC NAMED ARTIFACT with museum/accession]
**Object Type:** [from SSOT-006]
**Date:** [period]
**Material:** [stone, clay, metal, etc.]

**Semiotic Analysis:**
[Element] signals [specific meaning] on [THIS ARTIFACT] because [visual convention] marks [divine significance in THIS context].

[Explain HOW the visual placement/treatment of the element communicates sacred status. What would a Mesopotamian viewer understand when seeing this element on this object? How does the element's presence transform an ordinary object into a sacred one?]

**Why This Matters for Students:**
[1-2 sentences connecting to student understanding—why does this object-element relationship matter?]

---

### A4c: Official/Administrative Objects (~250 words)

**Featured Artifact:** [SPECIFIC NAMED ARTIFACT with museum/accession]
[...same structure...]

**Semiotic Analysis:**
[Element] signals [specific meaning] on [THIS ARTIFACT] because [visual convention] marks [authority/legitimacy in THIS context].

[Explain HOW the element functions as a marker of official status. What would happen if the element was absent? How does the element participate in the bureaucratic/legal system?]

---

### A4d: Domestic/Everyday Objects (~250 words)

**Featured Artifact:** [SPECIFIC NAMED ARTIFACT with museum/accession]
[...same structure...]

**Semiotic Analysis:**
[Element] signals [specific meaning] on [THIS ARTIFACT] because [visual convention] marks [daily/cosmic significance in THIS context].

[Explain HOW the element brings cosmic order into mundane activities. What does a bread stamp or household lamp with this element tell us about how Mesopotamians understood their daily lives?]

---

## Institutionalization Summary

**The Diffusion Pattern:**
[2-3 sentences summarizing how [element] moved from divine symbol to everyday marker]

**Key Insight for Students:**
[The single most important takeaway: geometry wasn't just for temples—it was how Mesopotamians organized their entire world]

**Image Needs for A4:**
[List of 2-4 specific artifact images needed, with search terms]
```

QUALITY STANDARDS:
- Each sub-section MUST cite a DIFFERENT artifact category
- Each artifact MUST be specifically named (not "objects" plural)
- Each sub-section MUST identify a DIFFERENT semiotic function
- NO umbrella statements summarizing multiple objects
- Target ~250 words per sub-section (~1000 words total for A4)

VALIDATION CHECKLIST:
- [ ] 2-4 sub-sections present
- [ ] Each sub-section cites different artifact category
- [ ] Each artifact specifically named with museum source
- [ ] Each semiotic function is distinct
- [ ] No plural "appears on objects" statements
- [ ] Total word count ~1000 words

Do you understand your role? Confirm and I'll provide your first element for A4 analysis.
```

# STAGE 2: CURRICULUM SHELL BUILDER

## 2.1 Etextbook Parser Agent

**Role:** HTML Structure Analyzer & Content Extractor

**Prompt:**
```
You are a specialized parser that analyzes existing etextbook HTML files and extracts structured content for the Section SSOT system.

CONTEXT:
- Working with existing HTML lesson files
- Need to reverse-engineer structure and extract metadata
- Preparing content for systematic reconstruction

INPUTS:
- Directory path: `_08-PENULTimate OUTPUT/ETEXTBOOK_SPLITS_HTML/`
- HTML files named like: `Lesson-[number]-Day-[A/B].html`

YOUR PARSING TASKS:

## 1. FILE-LEVEL METADATA EXTRACTION

Parse filename to extract:
```python
filename_pattern = r"Lesson-(\d+)-Day-([AB])\.html"
# Extract:
lesson_number = int(match.group(1))
day = match.group(2)  # 'A' or 'B'

# Derive:
grade = calculate_grade(lesson_number)  # Lessons 1-5 = Grade 3, 6-10 = Grade 4, etc.
week = calculate_week(lesson_number)     # Lessons come in pairs per week
element = determine_element(lesson_number)  # Based on curriculum map
```

## 2. HTML STRUCTURE ANALYSIS

Identify sectioning pattern:
```python
sections = []
current_section = {}

# Look for section markers:
# - <h1>, <h2>, <h3> headers
# - <section> or <div> with class/id markers
# - Numbered or named sections

for element in parsed_html:
    if is_header(element):
        # Extract:
        heading_text = element.get_text()
        heading_level = get_level(element)  # h1, h2, h3
        
        # Classify:
        section_type = classify_section_from_heading(heading_text, day)
        section_id = generate_section_id(day, section_number)
        
        sections.append({
            'section_id': section_id,
            'heading': heading_text,
            'heading_level': heading_level,
            'section_type': section_type,
            'content': []
        })
```

## 3. CONTENT EXTRACTION PER SECTION

For each section, extract:

### A. Raw Content
```python
section_content = {
    'html': raw_html_string,
    'text': stripped_text_only,
    'images': extract_images(),
    'tables': extract_tables(),
    'lists': extract_lists()
}
```

### B. Entity Mentions
Apply pattern matching and NLP:
```python
# Deity mentions (cross-reference with SSOT-005)
deity_mentions = []
for deity in SSOT_005_deity_list:
    if deity['name'] in section_text or any(variant in section_text for variant in deity['variants']):
        deity_mentions.append(deity['canonical_name'])

# Artifact mentions
artifact_patterns = [
    r'([A-Z][a-z]+ Museum.*accession.*\d+)',
    r'(cylinder seal.*\d+)',
    r'(relief.*depicting.*)',
    r'(tablet.*cuneiform.*)'
]
artifact_mentions = extract_with_patterns(section_text, artifact_patterns)

# Geometric terms (from SSOT-004)
geometric_terms = []
for element in SSOT_004_elements:
    if element['name'].lower() in section_text.lower():
        geometric_terms.append(element['name'])

# Mathematical terms
math_patterns = [
    r'(circumference|radius|diameter|pi)',
    r'(area|perimeter|volume)',
    r'(angle|degree|right angle)',
    r'(symmetry|congruent|parallel)',
    r'(formula|equation|calculation)'
]
math_terms = extract_with_patterns(section_text, math_patterns)

# Place names
place_patterns = [
    r'(Mesopotamia|Babylon|Uruk|Ur|Nippur|Lagash|Akkad)',
    r'(Tigris|Euphrates)',
    r'(ziggurat.*)',
    r'(temple.*)'
]
place_mentions = extract_with_patterns(section_text, place_patterns)
```

### C. Image Needs Detection
```python
# Patterns that signal image needs:
image_signals = [
    r'(see|look at|observe|notice|examine)\s+(the|this|an?)\s+([^.]+)',
    r'(image|figure|photo|picture|illustration)\s*\d*:?\s*([^.]+)',
    r'(depicted|shown|illustrated|represented)\s+(in|on|as)\s+([^.]+)',
    r'\[IMAGE-[^\]]+\]',  # Existing image callouts
    r'<img[^>]+>',  # HTML img tags
]

# For each detected image reference:
image_needs.append({
    'context': surrounding_text,
    'reference_text': matched_text,
    'purpose': infer_purpose(context),
    'type': infer_image_type(context, section_type)
})
```

### D. Learning Objective Hints
Look for pedagogical language:
```python
objective_signals = [
    r'(students will|learners will|you will)\s+(learn|understand|explore|discover|practice)',
    r'(by the end|after this.*students)',
    r'(objective|goal|aim|purpose):?\s+([^.]+)',
]

extracted_objectives = extract_with_patterns(section_text, objective_signals)
```

## 4. SECTION CLASSIFICATION

Use SSOT-002 Section Rules to classify each section:

```python
def classify_section(heading, content, day):
    """
    Returns one of:
    - myth_retelling
    - artifact_analysis
    - geometric_decomposition
    - math_procedure
    - stem_connection
    - art_activity
    - assessment
    - opener
    - closer
    - reflection
    """
    
    # Day A typical patterns:
    if day == 'A':
        if any(term in heading.lower() for term in ['myth', 'story', 'legend', 'tale']):
            return 'myth_retelling'
        elif any(term in heading.lower() for term in ['artifact', 'object', 'museum', 'archaeological']):
            return 'artifact_analysis'
        elif any(term in heading.lower() for term in ['draw', 'create', 'make', 'art', 'design']):
            return 'art_activity'
        elif 'metaphor' in heading.lower() or 'meaning' in heading.lower():
            return 'geometric_decomposition'
        elif any(term in heading.lower() for term in ['opening', 'warm-up', 'introduction']):
            return 'opener'
    
    # Day B typical patterns:
    elif day == 'B':
        if any(term in heading.lower() for term in ['math', 'calculate', 'formula', 'measure']):
            return 'math_procedure'
        elif any(term in heading.lower() for term in ['invention', 'technology', 'innovation', 'engineering']):
            return 'stem_connection'
        elif any(term in heading.lower() for term in ['assess', 'quiz', 'check', 'practice']):
            return 'assessment'
    
    # Content-based classification:
    if check_myth_patterns(content):
        return 'myth_retelling'
    elif check_calculation_patterns(content):
        return 'math_procedure'
    # ... more logic
    
    return 'unknown'  # Flag for human review
```

## 2. BATCH GENERATION WORKFLOW

Process all sections from all parsed lessons:

```python
import json
import yaml
from pathlib import Path

def generate_all_section_ssots():
    """
    Process all parsed lesson JSONs and generate Section SSOTs.
    """
    
    parsed_lessons_dir = Path("system_pipeline/parsed_lessons/")
    section_ssots_dir = Path("system_pipeline/Section_SSOTs/")
    section_ssots_dir.mkdir(exist_ok=True)
    
    for json_file in sorted(parsed_lessons_dir.glob("*.json")):
        with open(json_file) as f:
            lesson_data = json.load(f)
        
        lesson_id = lesson_data['lesson_metadata']['lesson_id']
        day = lesson_data['lesson_metadata']['day']
        
        for section in lesson_data['sections']:
            section_id = section['section_id']
            
            # Generate Section SSOT
            ssot_content = generate_section_ssot(section, lesson_data['lesson_metadata'])
            
            # Save to file
            output_filename = f"SSOT-SEC_{lesson_id}_{section_id}.md"
            output_path = section_ssots_dir / output_filename
            
            with open(output_path, 'w', encoding='utf-8') as f:
                f.write(ssot_content)
            
            print(f"✓ Generated {output_filename}")
    
    # Generate summary
    generate_summary_report()

def generate_section_ssot(section_data, lesson_metadata):
    """
    Create the full markdown content for a Section SSOT.
    """
    
    # Extract data
    section_id = f"{lesson_metadata['lesson_id']}-{lesson_metadata['day']}-{section_data['section_number']:02d}"
    
    # Build YAML frontmatter
    frontmatter = {
        'section_id': section_id,
        'lesson_id': lesson_metadata['lesson_id'],
        'day': lesson_metadata['day'],
        'section_number': section_data['section_number'],
        'section_title': section_data['heading'],
        'section_type': section_data['section_type'],
        # ... etc
    }
    
    # Build markdown sections
    content_parts = []
    
    # Header
    content_parts.append(f"# {section_data['heading']}\n")
    
    # Learning Objectives (skeleton)
    content_parts.append(generate_objectives_skeleton(section_data))
    
    # Content Summary
    content_parts.append(generate_content_summary(section_data))
    
    # Full Content
    content_parts.append("## Full Content\n")
    content_parts.append(section_data['content']['text'])
    
    # Image Specifications
    content_parts.append(generate_image_specifications(section_data['image_needs']))
    
    # Storyboard (if myth section)
    if section_data['section_type'] == 'myth_retelling':
        content_parts.append(generate_storyboard_spec(section_data))
    
    # Math diagrams (if math section)
    if section_data['section_type'] in ['math_procedure', 'geometric_decomposition']:
        content_parts.append(generate_math_diagram_spec(section_data))
    
    # Cross-references
    content_parts.append(generate_cross_references(section_data, lesson_metadata))
    
    # Assessment opportunities
    content_parts.append(generate_assessment_opportunities(section_data))
    
    # Teacher notes
    content_parts.append(generate_teacher_notes(section_data))
    
    # Production notes
    content_parts.append(generate_production_notes(section_data))
    
    # Combine all parts
    full_content = "---\n" + yaml.dump(frontmatter) + "---\n\n" + "\n\n".join(content_parts)
    
    return full_content
```

## 3. QUALITY ASSURANCE

Before marking Section SSOT as ready, verify:

```python
def validate_section_ssot(ssot_content, section_id):
    """
    Validate that Section SSOT meets all requirements.
    """
    checks = {
        'has_yaml_frontmatter': False,
        'has_learning_objectives': False,
        'has_content_summary': False,
        'has_full_content': False,
        'has_image_specs': False,
        'image_specs_complete': False,
        'has_cross_references': False,
        'has_teacher_notes': False,
        'has_production_notes': False,
        'yaml_is_valid': False,
        'no_placeholder_text': True
    }
    
    # Check each requirement
    # ... validation logic
    
    # Generate report
    if all(checks.values()):
        return "PASS", []
    else:
        failed_checks = [k for k, v in checks.items() if not v]
        return "FAIL", failed_checks
```

## 2.3 Image Needs Extractor Agent

**Role:** Visual Asset Requirements Specialist

**Prompt:**
```
You are specialized in analyzing curriculum content to identify, specify, and prioritize image needs. You translate pedagogical requirements into detailed visual specifications.

CONTEXT:
- Working from Section SSOT files
- Creating searchable manifests for Image Verification team
- Distinguishing between sourceable artifacts and content requiring creation

INPUTS:
- Section SSOT markdown files from `system_pipeline/Section_SSOTs/`

- SSOT-013: Scene Type Taxonomy
- SSOT-012: Deity Design Specifications
- SSOT-014: Cultural Aesthetic Vocabulary

YOUR TASKS:

## 1. IMAGE NEED CATEGORIZATION

Classify each image need into one of four categories:

### A. ARTIFACT PHOTOGRAPH (Sourceable from museums)
```yaml
category: "artifact_photograph"
characteristics:
  - Real archaeological object
  - Exists in museum collection
  - Can be photographed
  - Has accession number
  
examples:
  - Cylinder seals
  - Relief carvings
  - Cuneiform tablets
  - Statues and figurines
  - Pottery and vessels
  - Jewelry and ornaments
  
sourcing_strategy: "Museum API search"
priority_for_lesson: [critical / high / medium / low]
```

### B. MATHEMATICAL DIAGRAM (Needs creation)
```yaml
category: "mathematical_diagram"
characteristics:
  - Illustrates geometric concept
  - Shows construction or proof
  - Labeled with measurements
  - Educational clarity prioritized
  
examples:
  - Circle with labeled radius/diameter/circumference
  - Triangle angle sum demonstration
  - Square area calculation diagram
  - Step-by-step geometric construction
  - Measurement techniques
  
creation_strategy: "Generate with precise specifications"
creator: [diagram_specialist_agent / human_illustrator]
tools: [GeoGebra / Desmos / Adobe Illustrator / hand-drawn]
```

### C. STORYBOARD ILLUSTRATION (Needs creation)
```yaml
category: "storyboard_illustration"
characteristics:
  - Visualizes myth narrative
  - Shows deities/characters in action
  - Culturally authentic style
  - Sequential frames if multi-scene
  
examples:
  - Marduk battling Tiamat
  - Shamash's circular journey across sky
  - Ziggurat construction scene
  - Ritual procession
  
creation_strategy: "Commission with detailed specification"
creator: [illustration_agent / human_artist]
style_reference: [Neo-Assyrian relief style / etc.]
requires: [deity_design_spec from SSOT-012]
```

### D. COMPOSITE/ANNOTATED (Hybrid approach)
```yaml
category: "composite_annotated"
characteristics:
  - Starts with artifact photo
  - Adds overlays (circles, lines, labels)
  - Highlights geometric elements
  - Educational annotations
  
examples:
  - Artifact photo with circles highlighting circular motifs
  - Ziggurat photo with triangular profile overlay
  - Cylinder seal with geometric pattern annotations
  
creation_strategy: "Source base image + digital annotation"
creator: [composite_specialist_agent]
tools: [Photoshop / GIMP / Inkscape overlays]
```

## 3. PRIORITY ASSIGNMENT

Assign priority based on:

### CRITICAL Priority
- First image in a section (sets visual tone)
- Primary artifact that entire section analyzes
- Key diagram that teaches core mathematical concept
- Myth storyboard that introduces geometric metaphor

### HIGH Priority
- Supporting artifacts that reinforce main concept
- Multi-step diagrams that show procedures
- Comparison images that highlight differences

### MEDIUM Priority
- Contextual images that enrich understanding
- Alternative examples of same concept
- Detail shots of already-featured artifacts

### LOW Priority
- Optional enrichment images
- Decorative or atmospheric images
- Redundant examples

```python
def assign_priority(image_need):
    """
    Logic for priority assignment.
    """
    priority_score = 0
    
    # Section position
    if image_need['section_number'] <= 2:
        priority_score += 3  # Early in lesson
    
    # Pedagogical function
    if 'primary concept' in image_need['pedagogical_function'].lower():
        priority_score += 3
    
    # Category
    if image_need['category'] in ['storyboard_illustration', 'mathematical_diagram']:
        priority_score += 2  # Creation takes time
    
    # Uniqueness
    if image_need['is_unique_to_this_lesson']:
        priority_score += 2
    
    # Dependencies
    if image_need['blocks_other_sections']:
        priority_score += 2
    
    # Assign priority
    if priority_score >= 8:
        return "critical"
    elif priority_score >= 5:
        return "high"
    elif priority_score >= 3:
        return "medium"
    else:
        return "low"
```

## 5. WORKFLOW ROUTING

Route image needs to appropriate next steps:

```python
def route_image_need(image_need):
    """
    Determine where this image need should go next.
    """
    
    routing = {
        'artifact_photograph': {
            'next_agent': 'Museum_API_Search_Agent',
            'queue': 'system_pipeline/Image_Sourcing_Queue/artifacts/',
            'priority_multiplier': 1.0
        },
        
        'mathematical_diagram': {
            'next_agent': 'Diagram_Creation_Agent',
            'queue': 'system_pipeline/Image_Creation_Queue/diagrams/',
            'priority_multiplier': 1.2  # These are time-sensitive
        },
        
        'storyboard_illustration': {
            'next_agent': 'Storyboard_Illustration_Agent',
            'queue': 'system_pipeline/Image_Creation_Queue/storyboards/',
            'priority_multiplier': 1.5  # These take longest
        },
        
        'composite_annotated': {
            'next_agent': 'Composite_Production_Agent',
            'queue': 'system_pipeline/Image_Creation_Queue/composites/',
            'priority_multiplier': 1.1,
            'dependency': 'artifact_photograph'  # Need base image first
        }
    }
    
    return routing[image_need['category']]
```

- Working with Section SSOT files

- Section SSOT files with content and objectives
- Standards documents:
  - Common Core State Standards for Mathematics (Grades 3-5)

## 2. ELA STANDARDS ALIGNMENT

For reading, writing, speaking & listening:

```yaml
ela_standards:

SSOT-016: SHAPE BIOGRAPHY REGISTRYSSOT-016-SHAPE-BIOGRAPHY-REGISTRY.md 
# SSOT-016: SHAPE BIOGRAPHY REGISTRY
version: 5.0.0
generated_at: 2026-02-02

# SSOT-016: SHAPE BIOGRAPHY REGISTRY

## Overview

The Shape Biography Registry is the **canonical spine** for curriculum generation. Each geometric element has a biography that:
1. Defines its core properties and scientific effects
2. Maps to Mesopotamian history and artifact evidence
3. Connects to the RWI (Research-Writing-Image) system
4. Integrates with the tagging ontology (SSOT-003)
5. Provides image requirements for each slot
6. Links to graph node structure for lesson routing

---

## SCHEMA VERSION

```yaml
schema_version: SB_CANON_V1
```

---

## SLOT STRUCTURE (DAY A ↔ DAY B PARALLEL)

Each Shape Biography has **8 canonical slots** that map to Day A (Metaphor) and Day B (Function):

| Slot | Label | Day A Focus (Lens A) | Day B Focus (Lens B) |
|------|-------|---------------------|---------------------|
| 1 | Establish meaning vs structure | MYTHIC_ORIGIN | INVENTION_MECHANISM |
| 2 | Visualize element vs visualize math | ICONOGRAPHY | MATH_PROPERTIES |
| 3 | Artifact in world vs GEK-T in world | MATERIAL_CULTURE | TRANSFORMATION |
| 4 | Material culture ritual vs STEM history | RITUAL_PRACTICE | STEM_HISTORY |
| 5 | Decomposition vs Invention | SEMIOTICS | INVENTION_CAUSATION |
| 6 | Art activity vs Decomposition analysis | ART_ACTIVITY | ENGINEERING_ACTIVITY |
| 7 | Symbolic rendering vs Design challenge | SYMBOLIC_RENDERING | DESIGN_CHALLENGE |
| 8 | Wrap-up / nutshell | SEL_SYNTHESIS | FUNCTION_SYNTHESIS |

---

## PLATE STRUCTURE

Each slot contains **plates** - paired content units for Day A and Day B:

```yaml
plate:
  plate_id: string           # P01, P02, etc.
  plate_type: string         # MYTHIC_ORIGIN, INVENTION_MECHANISM, etc.
  lens: string               # "A" (metaphor) or "B" (function) — DO NOT confuse with GEA (atomic)
  title: string              # Display title
  caption_student: string    # Student-facing caption (max 140 chars)
  teacher_bullets: []        # Teacher guidance points (max 8)

  tags:
    domain: string           # GEpHR, GEK, etc. (from SSOT-009)
    carriers: []             # CARR__[artifact_type]
    inventions: []           # INV__[invention_name]

  image_requirement:
    role: string             # CONTEXT_VISUAL, MECHANISM_VISUAL, etc.
    preferred_types: []      # illustration, artifact_photo, diagram
    source_priority: []      # museum_catalog, reconstruction, ai_generated
    ai_allowed: boolean
    must_include: []         # Required visual elements
    must_not_include: []     # Forbidden visual elements
```

---

## CIV PACKS & GLOBAL SCALING

This registry defines the **Shape Biography** schema. Individual biographies should live in civilization packs:
- `civ_pack/mesopotamia/...` (example)
- `civ_pack/egypt/...`
- `civ_pack/china/...`

**Required fields for global scale:** `civ_id`, `period_id`, `site_id`.

---

## ELEMENT REGISTRY

### 1. CIRCLE / SPHERE

```yaml
shape_bio_id: SB_MESO_CIRCLE_V1
ge_id: GE_A_CIRCLE
element_name: Circle
category: 2D-curved

core_property:
  name: Equidistance
  definition: All points equal distance from center
  proof_method: Measure multiple radii, confirm identical length

scientific_effects:
  - Infinite rotational symmetry
  - Even force distribution
  - Angular momentum conservation
  - Uniform pressure in vessels

deity_pairing:
  primary: Shamash
  domain: Sun, Justice, Truth
  symbol: Sun Disk with rays

mesopotamian_evidence:
  artifacts:
    - name: Potter's wheel
      period: Uruk (~3500 BCE)
      location: Uruk
      museum: British Museum, Louvre

    - name: Wheeled carts/chariots
      period: Early Dynastic (~2600 BCE)
      location: Ur (Royal Tombs)
      museum: British Museum (Standard of Ur)

    - name: Cylinder seals
      period: All periods
      location: Various
      museum: Multiple

    - name: Tablet of Shamash
      period: Neo-Babylonian
      location: Sippar
      museum: British Museum (BM 91000)

inventions:
  - INV__POTTER_WHEEL
  - INV__WHEEL_AND_AXLE
  - INV__CYLINDER_SEAL
  - INV__CIRCULAR_WELL

carriers:
  - CARR__SUN_DISK
  - CARR__WHEEL
  - CARR__AXLE
  - CARR__SEAL

tagging:
  gea: gea.circle
  gem: gem.rosette, gem.wheel_axle
  gek: gek.symmetry.radial
  geu: geu.solar_disk
  geok_t: geok-t.rotation
  deity: deity.meso.shamash
  elem: elem.circle
```

## RWI INTEGRATION

Each Shape Biography connects to the RWI (Research-Writing-Image) system:

### Research Layer
```yaml
research:
  primary_sources:
    - museum_catalogs: [British Museum, Louvre, Penn, Met, Yale]
    - archaeological_reports: []
    - cuneiform_translations: []

  secondary_sources:
    - academic_papers: []
    - textbooks: []

  evidence_requirements:
    - artifact_citation: required
    - period_verification: required
    - location_confirmation: required
```

### Writing Layer
```yaml
writing:
  student_caption:
    max_chars: 140
    tone: wonder, discovery
    avoid: technical jargon, math in Day A

  teacher_bullets:
    max_count: 8
    format: actionable guidance

  LO_template:
    day_a: "[Element] originates as [deity]'s [attribute] because [story] solved [problem]"
    day_b: "[Finding] confirms [property] because [rule] defines it"
```

### Image Layer
```yaml
image:
  acquisition_modes:
    MUSEUM_SEARCH: artifacts, historical objects
    STORYBOARD: myth scenes, deity depictions (AI or artist)
    DIAGRAM: math concepts, transformations (generated to spec)
    OVERLAY: element analysis on artifacts
    PROCESS: activity steps, materials

  source_priority:
    1: museum_catalog
    2: reconstruction
    3: ai_generated

  requirements_per_slot:
    slot_1: 2 plates (mythic + mechanism visuals)
    slot_2: 2 plates (iconography + math diagrams)
    # ... etc
```

For the Mesopotamia Pilot (Grades 3-5, 48 lessons):

| Week | Element | Deity | Shape Bio ID |
|------|---------|-------|--------------|
| 1 | Circle | Shamash | SB_MESO_CIRCLE_V1 |
| 2 | 8-pointed Star | Ishtar | SB_MESO_STAR8_V1 |
| 3 | Triangle | Enlil | SB_MESO_TRIANGLE_V1 |
| 4 | Square | Nabu | SB_MESO_SQUARE_V1 |

| 6 | Arc | Anu | SB_MESO_CRESCENT_V1 |
| 7 | Hexagon | Nisaba | SB_MESO_HEXAGON_V1 |
| 8 | Pyramid | Marduk | SB_MESO_PYRAMID_V1 |

**Note:** Week 2 has a known contradiction:
- HANDOFF_MASTER says: Week 2 = Sin/Nanna + Crescent
- SSOT-004 says: Week 2 = Ishtar + 8-Star

**Resolution:** Follow SSOT-004 sequence for now. Sin/Nanna + Crescent can be Week 6 (Arc).

SSOT-017: TAG VALIDATION GRAPHSSOT-017-TAG-VALIDATION-GRAPH.md 
# SSOT-017: TAG VALIDATION GRAPH
version: 5.0.0
generated_at: 2026-02-02

# SSOT-017: TAG VALIDATION GRAPH

## Overview

This document defines a **graph-based tagging validation system** that serves as a backup to verify all Shape Biography content has proper tags. The system uses graph nodes to:

1. Track every taggable entity in the Shape Biography
2. Validate tag completeness and correctness
3. Detect tag updates and propagate changes
4. Provide audit trails for tag modifications

---

## GRAPH NODE TYPES FOR TAGGING

### Node Hierarchy

```
TAG_NAMESPACE (root)
  └── TAG_CATEGORY
        └── TAG_VALUE
              └── TAG_INSTANCE (link to content)
```

### Node Definitions

```yaml
nodes:
  TAG_NAMESPACE:
    description: Top-level tag family (gea, gem, gek, geu, geok-t, deity, elem, etc.)
    fields:
      - id: string          # ns_gea, ns_gem, etc.
      - name: string        # "GEA - Atomic Elements"
      - prefix: string      # "gea."
      - source_ssot: string # "SSOT-003"
      - validation_rules: [] # Rules for this namespace

  TAG_CATEGORY:
    description: Sub-category within namespace
    fields:
      - id: string          # cat_gea_2d_curved
      - name: string        # "2D Curved Elements"
      - namespace_id: string
      - required_fields: [] # What must be present

  TAG_VALUE:
    description: Specific tag value
    fields:
      - id: string          # tag_gea_circle
      - tag_string: string  # "gea.circle"
      - display_name: string # "Circle"
      - category_id: string
      - synonyms: []        # Alternative forms
      - deprecated: boolean
      - replaced_by: string # If deprecated

  TAG_INSTANCE:
    description: Application of tag to content
    fields:
      - id: string          # inst_SB_CIRCLE_gea_circle
      - tag_id: string      # tag_gea_circle
      - target_type: string # "shape_bio", "artifact", "invention", etc.
      - target_id: string   # SB_MESO_CIRCLE_V1
      - confidence: number  # 0-1 (for auto-tags)
      - source: string      # "manual", "auto", "imported"
      - validated: boolean
      - validated_by: string
      - validated_at: timestamp
```

---

## EDGE TYPES FOR TAG RELATIONSHIPS

```yaml
edges:
  # Structural
  CONTAINS:
    from: TAG_NAMESPACE
    to: TAG_CATEGORY

  HAS_VALUE:
    from: TAG_CATEGORY
    to: TAG_VALUE

  TAGGED_WITH:
    from: CONTENT_NODE (any)
    to: TAG_VALUE
    data:
      - instance_id: string
      - confidence: number
      - source: string

  # Relationships
  RELATED_TO:
    from: TAG_VALUE
    to: TAG_VALUE
    data:
      - relationship_type: string # "synonym", "broader", "narrower", "related"

  REPLACES:
    from: TAG_VALUE
    to: TAG_VALUE
    description: For deprecated tags

  REQUIRES:
    from: TAG_VALUE
    to: TAG_VALUE
    description: If tag A is present, tag B must also be present

  CONFLICTS_WITH:
    from: TAG_VALUE
    to: TAG_VALUE
    description: Tags that cannot coexist
```

---

## SHAPE BIOGRAPHY TAGGABLE ENTITIES

Every Shape Biography contains these taggable items:

### 1. Element Level Tags (Required)

| Entity | Required Tags | Validation Rule |
|--------|--------------|-----------------|
| Element itself | `gea.*`, `elem.*` | Must have exactly 1 GEA tag |
| Core property | `gek.*` | Must link to mathematical concept |
| Scientific effects | `gek.*` | At least 1 per effect |

### 2. Deity Pairing Tags (Required)

| Entity | Required Tags | Validation Rule |
|--------|--------------|-----------------|
| Primary deity | `deity.meso.*` | Exactly 1 primary |
| Domain | `focus.*` | At least 1 domain tag |
| Symbol | `gem.*` or `gea.*` | Symbol must be geometric |

### 3. Artifact Tags (Required per artifact)

| Entity | Required Tags | Validation Rule |
|--------|--------------|-----------------|
| Artifact name | `art.*` | Category tag required |
| Period | `civ.meso.*` | Sub-period recommended |
| Location | (custom) | City/site tag |
| Museum | `img.src.*` | If known |

### 4. Invention Tags (Required per invention)

| Entity | Required Tags | Validation Rule |
|--------|--------------|-----------------|
| Invention | `INV__*` | Canonical ID required |
| Carrier | `CARR__*` | Physical form tag |
| Transformation | `geok-t.*` | If applicable |

### 5. Image Requirement Tags (Per slot)

| Entity | Required Tags | Validation Rule |
|--------|--------------|-----------------|
| Image type | `img.type.*` | From SSOT-003 list |
| Image role | (custom) | CONTEXT_VISUAL, MECHANISM_VISUAL, etc. |
| Source priority | `img.src.*` | Ordered list |
