**Part 2: The Improved SSOT / Static HTML Structure**

To make this "best functionality possible," the documentation must be **operational**, not just descriptive. I have added sections for **Observability**, **Evaluation**, and **State Management**.

**1. System Architecture (The "Mental Model")**

- **High-Level Diagram:**
- **The "Flywheel" Concept:** Explain how Engine 4's outputs feed back into Engine 1 (images become new data points).
- **Global Schema Registry:** A link to the master JSON definitions for "Inventions," "Mythology," and "Geometric Shapes."

**2. Engine Detail Pages (Operational View)**

_Use this structure for every Engine page to ensure developers have what they need._

- **Mission & Scope:** (e.g., "Engine 1 turns unstructured PDFs into a GraphDB.")
- **The "Black Box" Input/Output:**

- _Strict Input Schema:_ (e.g., .pdf, .txt, URL)
- _Strict Output Schema:_ (e.g., GraphNode JSON object with ID, Vector, Edges)

- **Pipeline Logic (The "How"):**

- Step 1: Ingestion/Chunking Strategy (e.g., Semantic vs. Fixed).
- Step 2: Processing (e.g., "o1 Reasoning for entity extraction").
- Step 3: Validation (e.g., "Check if 'Triangle' is connected to 'Pyramid'").

- **Tooling & MCP Servers:** List of specific tools this engine calls (e.g., search_tool, vector_db_query).
- **Evaluation (Crucial):**

- _Success Metric:_ How do we know it worked? (e.g., "95% factual accuracy on extracted dates.")
- _Eval Set:_ Link to the "Golden Dataset" used to test this engine.

**3. The "Interlocks" (Handoff Protocols)**

_This is where systems usually break. Document the "Glues."_

- **State Objects:** Define the JSON object passed between engines.

- _Example:_ ProjectState { current_topic: "Pyramids", completed_research: boolean, image_assets: [] }

- **Failure Handlers:** What happens if Engine 2 (Writing) fails? Does it retry? Does it ask a human?

**4. Governance & Observability (The "Control Tower")**

- **Tracing Strategy:** How to trace a request ID from Engine 1 all the way to Engine 4. (e.g., "All logs must include trace_id").
- **Cost Controls:** Token limits per engine run.
- **Human-in-the-Loop Gates:** Specific points where a human _must_ click "Approve" (e.g., before publishing the Wiki page).

**5. Roadmap & "Eval Flywheel"**

- **Current Limitations:** (e.g., "Graph generation is slow on large PDFs.")
- **The "Eval Flywheel":** Process for gathering failure cases -> adding them to the test set -> re-running the prompts.

- _Reference:_ [Building resilient prompts using an evaluation flywheel](https://developers.openai.com/cookbook/examples/evaluation/building_resilient_prompts_using_an_evaluation_flywheel/)

This is a strong foundation, but it relies on "baseline" patterns. To achieve the "best functionality detail possible," you need to move from **standard implementation** (basic RAG, simple prompting) to **advanced orchestration** (Reasoning models, Swarm patterns, and generative UI).

Below is the **upgraded architecture**. I have replaced generic cookbook references with high-performance patterns (e.g., using o1/o3 for logic, Swarm for handoffs, and Structured Outputs for recursive UI).

  

**System Architecture: The "reasoning-First" Upgrade**

**Engine 1 — GraphNode Ingestion (The "Cortex")**

_Goal: Move from "searching files" to "understanding relationships."_

- **Upgrade 1: Knowledge Graph Extraction with Reasoning Models**  
    Instead of simple chunking, use **Reasoning Models (o1/o3-mini)** during ingestion to extract entities and relationships into a structured graph format (Neo4j/NetworkX) before vectorizing. This solves the "reasoning across documents" problem.

- _Pattern:_ **Temporal Knowledge Graph Extraction**.
- _Detail:_ The model reads a PDF and outputs a JSON edge-list: [{"source": "Clause 4", "relationship": "modifies", "target": "Clause 1", "condition": "if date > 2024"}].

- **Upgrade 2: Hierarchical & Hybrid Search**  
    Don't rely on just one vector store. Use a "Parent-Child" retrieval pattern.

- _Functionality:_ Search big chunks (Parents) for context, but retrieve specific paragraphs (Children) for precision. Combine **Keyword Search** (BM25) with **Semantic Search** (Cosine Similarity) for exact match accuracy (e.g., part numbers).

  

_Visualizes raw text passing through an o3-mini model which outputs structured nodes/edges, which are then stored in both a Vector DB (for similarity) and a Graph DB (for connectivity)._

**Engine 2 — RWI (Research / Writing / Integration)**

_Goal: Move from "collaborating agents" to "deliberate handoffs."_

- **Upgrade 1: Swarm Architecture (Handoffs > Routing)**  
    Replace generic "multi-agent" with the **Swarm Pattern**. Agents shouldn't just "talk"; they must explicitly **transfer control** like a relay race.

- _Functionality:_ The Triage Agent does not summarize; it hands off the entire context object to the Deep Research Agent, which hands off to the Critique Agent.
- _Key Detail:_ Use context_variables to maintain state (e.g., research_depth="high", tone="academic") across handoffs.

- **Upgrade 2: The "Reasoning Loop" (Plan -> Execute -> Reflect)**  
    Use **o3-mini** to write a research plan _before_ calling tools.

- _Pattern:_ **Reasoning for Data Validation**.
- _Detail:_ The agent generates a hidden "scratchpad" step where it critiques its own search queries before executing them, reducing noise.

**Engine 3 — Wiki / Lesson Builder (The "Renderer")**

_Goal: Move from "HTML generation" to "Recursive Generative UI."_

- **Upgrade 1: Recursive Component Generation**  
    Don't generate full HTML pages. Generate **React/Component Trees** using **Structured Outputs**.

- **Upgrade 2: Streaming UI**  
    Stream the structure. As the model "thinks," the UI skeleton appears, and content fills in real-time. This reduces perceived latency for long lesson generation.

This is a strong foundational architecture for a standard RAG (Retrieval-Augmented Generation) pipeline, but to achieve a **"self-learning and aggregating open universe ontology,"** the current plan has a critical gap: **It is linear.**

The current plan ingests data $\rightarrow$ processes it $\rightarrow$ outputs it.

To make it _self-learning_, you need a **Cyclical Graph Architecture**. The system must be able to read a document, realize the ontology is missing a concept (e.g., a specific Mesopotamian distinct geometric form like the _Rod and Ring symbol_), creates a new tag for it, and updates the "master brain" so future documents can use that tag.

Here is the improved technical architecture, specifically upgrading the **Engine definitions** and the **SSOT structure** to support _GraphRAG_ (Graph-based RAG) and _Dynamic Ontology Evolution_.

  

**Core Architectural Upgrade: The "Graph Brain"**

Instead of just a Vector Store (Engine 1), you need a **Knowledge Graph + Vector Hybrid**.

- **Old Way:** Document comes in $\rightarrow$ Chunks stored in Vector DB.
- **New Way:** Document comes in $\rightarrow$ LLM extracts Entities & Relationships $\rightarrow$ Updates Graph DB (e.g., Neo4j or NetworkX) $\rightarrow$ _Then_ chunks are stored.

  

**Engine Improvements (The Functionality Detail)**

Here is how to upgrade the OpenAI Cookbook mappings to support your specific "Mesopotamian Geometry" Semantic Review.

**Engine 1 — The Ontological Ingestor (GraphRAG)**

_Goal: Turn the "folder drop" into structured data nodes, not just text chunks._

- **Current Plan:** Simple File Search (Vector RAG).
- **The Upgrade:** **Recursive Entity Extraction via Structured Outputs.**

- **The Logic:** When a file drops, the LLM shouldn't just read it. It must extract specific entities (e.g., "Ishtar Gate") and relationships (e.g., "Ishtar Gate" _contains_ "Rosette Geometry").
- **The Cookbook Replacement:** **Structured Outputs for Entity Extraction**.

- _Why:_ You define a schema (Entity, Relationship, Category). The model enforces this schema.

- **The "Self-Learning" Mechanism:**

- Implement a "New Concept Candidate" protocol. If the model finds a geometric term that does not exist in your current Tag List, it flags it as status: candidate.
- _Functionality:_ A nightly batch job reviews candidate tags. If they appear $>3$ times, they are promoted to the permanent ontology.

**Engine 2 — The Semantic Reviewer (Reasoning & Context)**

_Goal: The Deep Review for "Mesopotamian Geometry" nuances._

- **Current Plan:** Standard Agents / Summarization.
- **The Upgrade:** **Reasoning Models (o1/o3) for Deep Semantic Analysis.**

- **The Logic:** Standard GPT-4o might miss the subtle connection between _astronomy_ and _ritual geometry_. You need "Reasoning" models to traverse the graph.
- **New Function:** **Multi-Step Semantic Routing.**

- _Step 1 (Vision):_ If the input is an image, use **GPT-4o with Vision** to describe the geometry (e.g., "Identify the hexagonal symmetry in this artifact").
- _Step 2 (Reasoning):_ Pass that description to **o1/o3** with the prompt: _"Analyze this geometry against Mesopotamian mythological frameworks. Does this shape correspond to specific astral bodies or deities?"_

- **Cookbook Addition:** **Reasoning with o1**.

- _Ref:_ [OpenAI o1 Reasoning Guide](https://platform.openai.com/docs/guides/reasoning) (Crucial for connecting abstract concepts like "myth" to "math").

**Engine 3 — The Aggregator (Dynamic Wiki)**

- Engine 3 should run a "Gap Scan." It looks at the Graph and says: _"We have 50 nodes on 'Architecture' but only 2 on 'Ritual Geometry'. Prompting Engine 2 to search specifically for Ritual Geometry to fill this gap."_

- **Cookbook Upgrade:** **Structured Outputs for UI Components**.

3. Engine 4 analyzes those fragments with Vision API to understand the "Material Cultural" texture.
4. Engine 4 writes a highly detailed prompt for DALL-E 3 that combines the _text description_ with the _visual texture parameters_ of the real artifacts.

**Summary of the "Delta" (What to change immediately)**

1. **Change the Trigger:** The folder drop shouldn't just trigger a "Review"; it should trigger an **Extraction**. Use **Structured Outputs** to force the AI to return data in a specific JSON format ({ "entity": "Ziggurat", "geometry_type": "fractal", "context": "ritual" }) rather than free text.
2. **Add the Feedback Loop:** Explicitly design a database table or JSON file called ontology_master.json. Engine 1 reads from it to know valid tags. Engine 2 writes to a temporary suggested_tags.json. A script merges them.
3. **Upgrade the Model:** Explicitly specify **o1 (Reasoning)** for the "Review" phase to handle the complex intersection of Math, Myth, and Art.

Would you like me to draft the **Structured Output Schema** (the JSON definition) for that "Semantic Review" so you can see exactly how the AI will tag the Mesopotamian content?

🧭 Implementation Plan (Engines + Tools)

Plan

Canonical implementation plan for each engine and tool.

# EUCLID Functionality Plan (Implementation)
Version: 1.0
Date: 2026-02-08

Purpose: Provide a clear implementation plan for each engine and tool in the Euclid system. This plan is aligned to SSOT v5 and intended to be copied into the static SSOT HTML.

Definitions
- Engine 1: GraphNode Ingestion and Knowledge Graph (Brain)
- Engine 2: RWI (Research, Writing, Image) and Semantic Control
- Engine 3: Wiki and Lesson Builder (Factory)
- Engine 4: Etextbook Assembly and Delivery (Distribution)

Success Criteria (Global)
- Every tool has defined inputs, outputs, and storage locations.
- Every engine has a complete flow with validation checkpoints.
- No step assumes hidden context.

---

Engine 1: GraphNode Ingestion (Brain)
Goal: Convert raw files into structured nodes, relationships, and semantic tags.

Inputs
- Raw files: PDF, MD, TXT, HTML, images
- SSOT registries: elements, deities, artifact types
- Tagging ontology and section rules

Outputs
- Graph nodes: ContentUnit, Artifact, Image, Deity, Element
- Relationships: HAS_SECTION, REFERENCES, SOURCED_FROM, ALIGNS_WITH
- Ingestion log with timestamps and source paths

Core Flow
1. Detect new files in intake folders.
2. Normalize file text (OCR if needed).
3. Extract entities and relationships using a structured schema.
4. Validate against SSOT registries and tag ontology.
5. Write nodes and relationships to Graph DB.
6. Store raw file metadata and source path in ingestion log.

Validation
- Reject nodes missing required fields.
- Reject tags outside SSOT-003 Tagging Ontology.
- Enforce unique node IDs per source hash.

Minimum Implementation Checklist
- File watcher or manual ingest CLI.
- Parser for PDF and MD.
- Schema for node and relationship output.
- Graph persistence (Neo4j or equivalent).
- Ingestion log table or JSONL file.

---

Engine 2: RWI (Research, Writing, Image)
Goal: Produce validated content, create image needs, and enforce semantic integrity.

Inputs
- Graph nodes and tags from Engine 1.
- SSOT section rules and LO framework.
- Image requirements from SSOT-010.

Outputs
- Research notes with citations.
- Drafted section content with structured fields.
- Image needs list per section.
- Validation outcomes for content and tags.

Core Flow
1. Research agent gathers sources for a lesson or section.
2. Writer agent produces section drafts per SSOT section rules.
3. Image agent generates image needs per section.
4. Validator agent checks LO, tags, and section constraints.
5. Store approved drafts in the content repository.

Validation
- Every drafted section must pass SSOT-002 rules.
- Every claim must be backed by a source or marked as unverified.
- Every image need must contain type, purpose, and placement target.

Minimum Implementation Checklist
- Research orchestration with sources list.
- Section drafting templates.
- Image needs generator.
- Content validation rules.
- Output format in JSON or YAML for Engine 3.

---

Engine 3: Wiki and Lesson Builder (Factory)
Goal: Assemble finalized lesson artifacts and create wiki views.

Inputs
- Approved drafts from Engine 2.
- Image assets and image needs.
- SSOT templates for lesson overview.

Outputs
- Lesson packages with 17 sections.
- Wiki pages per node (Element, Deity, Artifact, Lesson).
- Ready to render JSON structures.

Core Flow
1. Pull section drafts and images for a lesson.
2. Populate lesson templates with structured data.
3. Assemble crosslinks between wiki nodes.
4. Export JSON for rendering and publish to storage.

Validation
- Lesson must contain all required sections.
- Each section must have LO and required tags.
- Every image placement must resolve to a stored asset.

Minimum Implementation Checklist
- Lesson assembler from JSON and templates.
- Wiki page generator.
- Crosslink generator based on tags.
- Storage location for lesson packages.

---

Engine 4: Etextbook Assembly and Delivery (Distribution)
Goal: Render lessons and assets into a final eTextbook interface.

Inputs
- Lesson packages from Engine 3.
- UI specs and layouts.
- Image assets and credits.

Outputs
- Static HTML or SPA pages.
- Teacher script and slideshow packages.
- Asset manifests for deployment.

Core Flow
1. Render lesson packages into HTML or component layouts.
2. Inject images, metadata, and credit lines.
3. Build navigation structure by grade, unit, lesson.
4. Export final assets to deployment folder.

Validation
- Every rendered lesson passes content completeness check.
- Navigation does not include broken links.
- Image credits file is complete.

Minimum Implementation Checklist
- Render pipeline from JSON to HTML.
- Layout templates for teacher and student views.
- Asset manifest generator.
- Deployment folder structure.

---

Tool Plans (Cross-Engine)

Tool: Image Sourcing
Purpose: Retrieve and normalize museum images for lesson placement.
Inputs: Image needs list, artifact tags, source registry.
Outputs: Image candidates with metadata and license.
Steps
1. Query museum APIs by tag and artifact.
2. Normalize responses to a single schema.
3. Rank results by relevance and license quality.
4. Store candidates and metadata.
5. Mark approved images for placement.

Tool: Image Validation
Purpose: Verify visual alignment and licensing.
Inputs: Candidate image, target section, tags.
Outputs: Approval or rejection with reasons.
Steps
1. Check license and source.
2. Validate content alignment via rules or vision model.
3. Store validation result.
4. Update image status.

Tool: Tagging System
Purpose: Apply consistent tags across content, artifacts, and images.
Inputs: Raw content, SSOT tagging ontology.
Outputs: Tag list and confidence score.
Steps
1. Extract candidate tags.
2. Validate tag membership in SSOT-003.
3. Store tags with source and confidence.

Tool: Lesson Registry
Purpose: Single list of all lessons and their status.
Inputs: Lesson packages from Engine 3.
Outputs: Index with status, coverage, readiness.
Steps
1. Register each lesson with metadata.
2. Track completeness and last update.
3. Expose a queryable index.

Tool: Ingestion Monitor
Purpose: Track all incoming files and their processing state.
Inputs: File system events and ingestion logs.
Outputs: Ingestion status dashboard.
Steps
1. Log file events.
2. Track ingestion success or failure.
3. Allow re-run for failed items.

Tool: Audit and Snapshot
Purpose: Provide system health summaries and evidence of functionality.
Inputs: Logs, lesson registry, image pipeline status.
Outputs: Audit reports and summary HTML.
Steps
1. Collect latest metrics.
2. Flag missing or failing components.
3. Publish dashboard updates.

---

Implementation Order (Recommended)
1. Engine 1 ingestion pipeline with SSOT registries.
2. Engine 2 research and section drafting.
3. Image sourcing and validation tools.
4. Engine 3 lesson builder.
5. Engine 4 rendering and deployment.
6. Audit and snapshot tooling.

Known Gaps Against SSOT
- Missing IMG, TPL, WRK, BOT, VAL, REF docs as defined in SSOT-000.
- Need explicit data contracts between engines.
- Need canonical storage paths for outputs.

End of Plan
